Decide the form together with the unconditional-existence question above, or drop the three barriers (new on this branch, so no live URL) and keep the results in the variant sections and Other results of `oblivious-ram.md`, moving their Notes there — _source: content/Barriers/no-oram-to-sublogarithmic-oram-ln18.md, content/Barriers/no-oram-to-sublogarithmic-balls-and-bins-oram-go96.md, content/Barriers/no-oram-to-sub-square-root-one-round-oram-cdh20.md_

- [ ] [Content] 3 relation statements arrived on `main` after the reductions audit ran and are still prose: YYS25 on `homomorphic-encryption.md` (IND-CCA1 section) and two Kha26 bullets on `secret-sharing.md`. Neither paper is on arXiv, and the eprint abstracts (2026/1756; 2026/1769, revised 2026-09-09) do not settle the pages. YYS25 stays blocked on its hypothesis and conclusion. The abstract says only "circular-secure LWE, which matches the assumptions needed for FHE with the basic chosen-plaintext security" and "batch arguments for NP and a new primitive called predicate extractable commitment". It does not say whose circular security is meant, for which key-dependent messages, or whether plain LWE is a separate hypothesis for the BARGs and the commitments, and it never names IV-CCA. So a hypothesis node (not `circular-secure-fhe`) and an IV-CCA node need the paper's definitions. PR #37's intake notes, written from the PDF, point to Corollary 4.1 (IV-CCA FHE from circular-secure LWE) and Theorem 4.1 (IND-CPA HE with strong evaluation correctness, a simulation-sound NIZK, a predicate extractable commitment from standard LWE, a somewhere-extractable index BARG). Kha26: the first bullet (the rank measure survives amortization; scoped to the rank measure and restated in share-size terms on 2026-11-10) is a size inequality for multi-target MSPs, not an edge between objects. It belongs in the Notes or Sketch of the barrier below, or stays prose. The second bullet fits the ORAM lower-bound pattern (`bar-oram-to-sublogarithmic-oram-ln18`: unconditional, class free, consequence contradiction). That barrier needs nodes the wiki lacks: secret sharing for general access structures (`secret-sharing.md` defines only $(t,n)$ threshold schemes; existence needs an ISN87 or BL88 reference stub), multilinear schemes over $\FF^k$ with average and maximum information ratio, and the impossible variant "perfect multilinear SS with information ratio $2^{o(n)}$ for every access structure". All of these must be defined as Kha26 defines them, which needs the PDF. PR #36's intake notes point to Theorem 3.1, Theorem 4.7 and Corollary 4.9. "Question 10.4 of Bei25" is in neither abstract: the 2026-08-21 one says only "answering a question of Beimel" and the current one drops it. PR #36's notes place it at Kha26 Corollary 4.9 — _source: git log 75f2542..043f406, PRs #36 and #37, content/Primitives/homomorphic-encryption.md, content/Primitives/secret-sharing.md_

- [x] [Math] Suspected error (audit 2026-08-21; fixed by 2026-09-27, high, review-only): Pessiland is mischaracterized. The world list defines "**Pessiland**: $\classP\neq \classNP$ but OWFs do not exist" (overlapping Heuristica), and the '## A note on hardness' section says: "This gap explains why Pessiland exists: a world where NP is hard in the worst case (so $\classP \neq \classNP$) but NP is easy on average — meaning random instances of NP problems are tractable — so OWFs cannot — _source: content/impagliazzos-five-worlds.md_
- [x] [Math] Suspected error (audit 2026-08-21; fixed by 2026-09-27, high, review-only): In '## Breaking up Cryptomania': "TDPs (equivalently, the existence of PKE or OT) unlock the full power of asymmetric cryptography." TDP, PKE, and OT are not known to be equivalent — TDP implies PKE and OT, but not conversely, and the GKM+00 paper cited in the same paragraph proves black-box separations among exactly these primitives. 'equivalently' is wrong as stated. — _source: content/impagliazzos-five-worlds.md_
- [x] [Math] Suspected error (audit 2026-08-21; fixed by 2026-09-27, medium, review-only): In '## Obfustopia': "iO ... can exist even in a world where $\classP = \classNP$: the definition of iO does not require any computational hardness beyond the existence of OWFs." Self-contradictory as written — if P = NP then OWFs do not exist, so 'beyond the existence of OWFs' cannot be the reason iO survives P = NP. The standard statement is that iO _by itself_ (without OWFs) implies no hardness — _source: content/impagliazzos-five-worlds.md_

LP07's eprint abstract (https://eprint.iacr.org/2008/049) says only that "the only usage of asymmetric cryptography is for running $O(1)$ oblivious transfers for each input bit (or for each bit of a statistical security parameter, whichever is larger)". Since the protocol garbles $s$ circuits, one OT per input bit suggests each OT carries that wire's keys in all $s$ circuits. CCG+20's eprint abstract (https://eprint.iacr.org/2019/216) says only that security "relies only on the existence of four round oblivious transfer", so whether its four-round MPC needs string OT from `four-round-ot` is still unknown. Neither paper has an arXiv version. - [x] [Math] `decisional-linear` is defined in a plain group, while GOS06b's NIZK and KSW08's IPPE need DLIN in a symmetric bilinear group: `red-bilinear-pairing-and-dlin-to-zap-gos06` lists `bilinear-pairing` as a co-hypothesis, but `red-dlin-to-nizk-gos06` and `red-dlin-to-ippe-ksw08` do not. Add the co-hypothesis (or a pairing-group DLIN variant) before any edge into `decisional-linear` lands (k-Lin ⇒ (k+1)-Lin gives DDH ⇒ DLIN group by group), which would otherwise derive NIZK for NP and IPPE from DDH — fixed 2026-11-10: red-dlin-to-nizk-gos06 (GOS06b) and red-dlin-to-ippe-ksw08 (OT12) now carry `bilinear-pairing` as a co-hypothesis beside the plain-group `decisional-linear`, matching their Statements and the GOS06b zap page, so a later DDH ⇒ DLIN edge cannot derive NIZK or IPPE from DDH alone; ids kept — _source: content/Reductions/bdh-to-nizk-gro16.md, content/Reductions/dlin-to-ippe-ksw08.md_

- [ ] [Math] `sis-to-pwpp-szz18` consumes the generic `sis` node, whose regime $m > n\log q$ includes super-polynomially small slack $m - n\log q$, where SIS is shown in PPP but not in PWPP — _source: content/Reductions/sis-to-pwpp-szz18.md_
- [ ] [Math] `subexponential-lwe` quantifies only prime $q \le \poly(n)$ (with $\alpha q \ge 2\sqrt n$). `polynomial-ratio-lwe` and `quasi-polynomial-lwe` quantify every $q \le 2^{\poly(n)}$, so no edge from sub-exponential LWE reaches them. Rechecked 2026-11-10 against BLPRS13 arXiv 1306.0281v1: its modulus switching does not close the gap. (a) Thm 3.1 with $G = I$ needs $\beta^2 \ge \alpha^2 + (4/\pi)\ln(2n(1+1/\varepsilon))(B/\min\{q,q'\})^2$. A normal-form secret $D_{\ZZ^n,\sqrt2\alpha q}$ (Lemma 2.12) has norm $\Theta(\alpha q\sqrt n)$ with high probability, so $B = \Omega(\alpha q\sqrt n)$; Cor 3.3 gives $\beta = C\alpha\sqrt{n\log(n/\delta)\log(m/\delta)}$. The target width is then $\beta q' = \Omega(\alpha q\sqrt{n\log n})$ whether $q' < q$ or $q' \ge q$, which is $\Omega(n\sqrt{\log n})$ from any source with $\alpha q \ge 2\sqrt n$. So the route does not reach targets with $2\sqrt n \le \alpha'q' \ll n\sqrt{\log n}$. (b) The output is LWE$_{\le\beta}$ (Def 2.14). Lemma 2.15 turns an exact-noise distinguisher of advantage $\varepsilon$ into a bounded-noise one of advantage $1/3$ with $\poly(m,1/\varepsilon,n,\log q)$ samples and time. At `quasi-polynomial-lwe`'s advantage $2^{-(\log n)^d}$ that sample count is quasi-polynomial, outside sub-exponential LWE's $m = \poly(n)$, so sample amplification is also needed. (c) BLPRS13 (Def 2.11) uses continuous Gaussians over $\mathbb{T}$; the wiki uses discrete Gaussians over $\ZZ_q$. BLPRS13's hardness 'for all moduli q' (Cor 3.3) starts from a power-of-2 modulus, not from prime $q \le \poly(n)$. Two ways to close this: find a source stating that hardness at prime $q \le \poly(n)$ implies hardness at every $q \le 2^{\poly(n)}$ with polynomial ratio, or have a maintainer re-align the modulus and width quantifiers of the three variants. BLNWW25 has no arXiv version, and its eprint PDF (2024/1663) is not readable here. Its abstract says only 'quasi-polynomial hardness of the learning with errors (LWE) assumption with a polynomial modulus-to-noise ratio', so its modulus regime is still unknown — _source: content/Assumptions/learning-with-errors.md_

- [ ] [FactCheck] PIR notes still unverified. Settled 2026-11-10 from eprint abstracts: FrodoPIR's benchmark (one million 1 KB elements), MR23's (256 entries from one million 256-byte entries), ZLTS23's bounds, single round trip, optimality and LWE, and ACLS18's query compression (up to 274×) and PBC speedup (up to 40×), all per the current eprint 2017/1142 abstract. Still open: (1) the hardness assumptions of SealPIR (BFV/ring-LWE), OnionPIR (BFV and RGSW), Spiral (Regev and GSW over a ring), FrodoPIR (LWE), and YPIR's 'LWE with ring-LWE response compression' (still on the page), none of which their abstracts name. (2) ACLS18 claims absent from the abstract. Still on the page: PBCs failing 'about one in a trillion multi-queries', with 'far fewer buckets' cutting network cost. Dropped from the page: SealPIR's query compressed 'into a single ciphertext', which plausibly holds only for queries that fit one ciphertext. The 'over 120×' figure from an older version was not seen. (3) Whether NP99's STOC version already states the PIR-to-SPIR transformation cited to NP05 (DOI-only). (4) The communication of DMO00's OT and of its PIR-to-SPIR reduction (Springer only; the abstract gives no bound). (5) NP05's construction behind the PIR-to-SPIR corollary and its communication (JoC, DOI-only, no abstract on the page) — _source: content/Primitives/single-server-private-information-retrieval.md, content/Reductions/symmetric-private-information-retrieval-single-server-to-ot.md_
- [ ] [Math] Single-server SPIR has no producing edge. The SPIR node asks for total communication at most $p(\secpar) \cdot n^c$ ($c < 1$) and fixes no security level, semi-honest or malicious. Two transformations cannot be typed yet: DMO00's communication-efficient reduction from any PIR to 1-out-of-$n$ OT, and NP05's PIR-to-SPIR transformation (reportedly $\log N$ executions of 1-out-of-2 OT and a PRF). No readable source gives their communication or security level. DMO00 is Springer-only and its abstract gives no bound. NP05 and NP99 are DOI-only with no abstracts. The OS07 survey PDF is behind the eprint bot check. HPY26 (arXiv 2607.06451v3 §2) mentions only generic PIR-to-SPIR via oblivious PRFs (FIPR05, ALP+21). Candidates, unchanged: `{X, ot} ⇒ single-server-symmetric-pir` (NP05) is a candidate only for X = `polylog-cpir` or `one-round-sublinear-cpir`, never `cpir` or `total-nontrivial-cpir`, whose bound may be $n - 1$ at a single $n$. `{one-round-sublinear-cpir} ⇒ single-server-symmetric-pir` (DMO00) is a candidate once DMO00's SPIR communication is read. `{polylog-cpir} ⇒ ...` (DMO00) also requires that DMO00 tolerate negligible error and a randomized server. From `total-nontrivial-cpir` it reaches the node's bound only if DMO00 turn a saving at a single $n$ into $p(\secpar) \cdot n^c$ at every $n$, which their abstract does not claim — _source: content/Primitives/single-server-private-information-retrieval.md_

- [ ] [Math] FHE ⇒ PIR needs $\Eval$ to be correct on the selection circuit $f_D$, of size $\Theta(n)$, for every $n$, because `polylog-cpir` is required for every $n$. The leveled and compact FHE nodes guarantee correctness only for polynomial-size circuits, so a scheme whose $\Eval$ fails above $2^{\sqrt{\secpar}}$ gates meets either node and breaks the PIR at $n = 2^\secpar$. Both FHE ⇒ `polylog-cpir` edges were removed for this gap: `red-he-to-cpir` (compact FHE, page `he-to-cpir.md`) and `red-leveled-fully-homomorphic-encryption-to-polylog-cpir`. Since then no LWE node derives `polylog-cpir`, `nontrivial-cpir` or `dv-delegation-for-p`. Fixing this needs papers this pass could not read: BGV12 (eprint 2011/277), BV14 (2013/541), CLTV15 (2014/882) and BHK17 (2016/459) are eprint PDFs with no arXiv version, Gen09 is DOI-only, and the abstracts give no correctness quantifier. BHK17's abstract says only 'any computational PIR scheme', with no database-size bound. Align one side. Either let the leveled node's correctness cover every circuit within the depth bound (check BGV12, BV14, Gen09) and the compact node's every circuit of any size (check Gen09, CLTV15; circular-secure FHE inherits it). Or restrict `polylog-cpir` to $n \le \poly(\secpar)$, checking BHK17's database size and the other consumers (`polylog-cpir-to-cpir`, `polylog-cpir-to-nontrivial-cpir`, `polylog-cpir-to-dv-delegation-for-p-bhk17`, Lip05's edge into the node). Once the compact node or `polylog-cpir` is aligned, restore `he-to-cpir` (Query runs $\KeyGen(1^\secpar)$). Once the leveled node or `polylog-cpir` is aligned, restore the leveled edge, with $\KeyGen(1^{\secpar'}, 1^L)$ at $\secpar' = \max(\secpar, \lceil \log n \rceil)$ and $L = c \secpar'$ — _source: content/Primitives/homomorphic-encryption.md, content/Primitives/single-server-private-information-retrieval.md_

- [ ] [FactCheck] Approximate HE: the CCPSS24 and CSBB24 sentences now follow their eprint abstracts; CCPSS24's attacks are scoped to 'all major exact FHE schemes' with loose correctness. Check LMSS22's Definitions 11 and 15 against the `statically-approximate-homomorphic-encryption` node: whether $\mathsf{Estimate}$ and $t_e$ may depend on the parameters given to $\KeyGen$; whether Definition 11's base scheme has deterministic decryption; whether the $t_i$ bound message magnitudes as well as errors; whether Definition 11's messages range over $\calM$. Check whether the tags are compact, and $t_e$ and $\mathsf{Estimate}$ efficiently computable uniformly in $(\secpar, L, p)$. Finally check LMSS22's noise width and precision loss, whether its theorem is for $q$-bounded IND-CPA$^{\mathrm{D}}$ with noise depending on $q$ (as `approximate-he-ind-cpa-d` assumes), and whether the parameters give negligible advantage for every polynomial $q$ under $\KeyGen(1^\secpar, 1^L, 1^p, 1^q)$ rather than a fixed bit-security level (the abstract separates a computational parameter c from a statistical one s). - [ ] [FactCheck] Somewhat homomorphic encryption: CHKV25's eprint abstract (2024/1760) confirms that the number of homomorphic operations is bounded a priori ($O(\log\secpar/\log\log\secpar)$ multiplications followed by $\poly(\secpar)$ additions), that ciphertexts before and after evaluation have length 'independent of the number of homomorphic operations that the scheme supports', and that the evaluation key grows with the complexity of the computation; both CHKV25 reduction pages now say so. - [ ] [FactCheck] Circular security, partly settled 2026-11-10 from eprint abstracts. Now recorded: Rot13's degree bound l >= c log p with c > 1; its full-key-recovery attack; the extension of both negative results to CCA-secure bit encryption; its black-box notion, which is reductions for the same scheme that use the scheme and the adversary as black boxes; its search-to-decision equivalence; KRW15's iO-based bit-encryption separation; and GKW17c's LWE-based public-key bit-encryption separation. Still open, needing full texts (all eprint PDFs, none on arXiv): (1) whether Rot13's black-box result is unconditional, and what the third of its three equivalent notions is, including whether the wiki's HE notion (IND-CPA given key-bit encryptions) is one of them. The black-box result itself cannot become a barrier page, because the class vocabulary has no 'fixed construction with black-box proof' class: fixed-construction would overclaim, and so would fully-black-box. (2) KRW15's exact assumptions; the barrier records `[io, owf]`, but the abstract names only iO. (3) KW16's LWE regime, and whether it gives one scheme for all cycle lengths or one per length. GKW17b's abstract implies prior work was per length. (4) GKW17a's LWE regime, and whether its private-key semantic security is multi-query CPA, the barrier's hypothesis `cpa-security`, or one-message security, in which case the hypothesis must be a one-message node. (5) GKW17c's LWE regime (the abstract says only that lockable obfuscation needs polynomially hard LWE, with no complexity leveraging), and which iO-based separations besides public-key bit encryption it translates, before a barrier with `conditional-on` LWE is added for public-key bit encryption — _source: content/Assumptions/circular-security.md, content/Barriers/no-pke-cpa-security-to-bit-circular-security-rot13.md, content/Barriers/no-pke-cpa-security-to-k-circular-security-krw15.md, content/Barriers/no-cpa-security-to-secret-key-bit-circular-security-gkw17a.md_

They need the eprint PDFs 1996/002, 2011/046 and 2013/454 (none of the three is on arXiv); the abstracts were read 2026-11-10. Settled by the abstracts: CDNO97 'propose constructions of schemes with polynomial deniability' and state that 'Analogous requirements can be formulated with respect to attacking the receiver and with respect to attacking both parties'. BNNO11 prove 'there does not exist any non-interactive receiver-deniable cryptosystem with better than polynomial security', hence no non-interactive bi-deniable PKE with better than polynomial security, with 'an explicit bound relating the security of the scheme to how efficient the scheme is in terms of key size' and constructions giving upper bounds in terms of key length; the BNNO11 bullet matches this apart from (d). Once Bol03 § 3 is read: if the theorem reduces to CDH, create content/Reductions/type-1-pairing-and-cdh-to-threshold-bls-bol03.md (kind: implication, hypotheses [type-1-pairing, cdh], conclusion threshold-bls, model rom, class unstated, source Bol03), with a Statement noting that Bol03 prove it in every gap Diffie–Hellman group, as type-1-pairing-and-cdh-to-multi-signature-kosk-bol03 does, and add the page to the sources of the Type 1 pairing [Design] item. If it instead reduces to the GDH/BLS signature's unforgeability through GJKR-style simulatability, create boneh-lynn-shacham-signature-to-threshold-bls-bol03.md with hypothesis boneh-lynn-shacham-signature, per the split-chains rule. Either way, put Bol03's corruption bound and key-generation model in the Statement, run generate-relations, and link the page from the sentence ending 'such as a group with a [[pairings#type-1|symmetric pairing]] in which CDH is hard.', using its title as the display text. — _source: content/Primitives/threshold-signature.md_

[93] is [[DEF+19 - On the Security of Two-Round Multi-Signatures|DEF+19]], whose abstract (https://eprint.iacr.org/2018/417) gives 'practical sub-exponential attacks' on the two-round Schnorr-based multi-signatures BCJ, MWLD, CoSi and MuSig. - [ ] [Math] threshold-signature § Robustness now has $\Game^{\mathrm{rob}}_{\Pi,\calA}(\secpar)$ (added 2026-11-10). It uses ts-uf's corruption, $\KeyGen$ and $\calO_{\Sign}$, completes unfinished sessions with unsent corrupted messages set to $\bot$, and counts a win iff a session with $|S \setminus C| \ge t+1$ has an honest party output $\bot$ or an invalid $\sigma$. The page also states, as standard, three consequences: for $n \ge 2t+1$, a session on all $n$ parties of a robust scheme outputs a valid signature; for $n \le 2t$, corrupting $t$ parties leaves the game nothing to check; and the non-interactive condition (every $t+1$ shares passing ShareVrfy combine into a valid signature) implies robustness. Remaining: compare with Sho00's own robustness definition, which was not read (eprint 1999/011 PDF behind the bot check; no arXiv version; the abstract says only 'unforgeable and robust'). If Sho00 defines robustness as that non-interactive condition, or with a bound on $n$ and $t$, cite Sho00 for it in § Robustness. — _source: content/Primitives/threshold-signature.md_

Restate it as 'unmodified DSA and ECDSA are <notion, e.g. UF-KOA / UF-CMA>-secure if DLOG is hard and the hash function satisfies <FKP16's stated conditions>, without assuming generic group behavior'. If FKP16 gives different conditions per notion, state each separately, with notions and hash conditions taken verbatim from FKP16's theorems. Do not fill this in from memory. — fixed 2026-11-10: per Kre21 arXiv v5 Sec. 5.1 and Thms 30 and 33, the oracle is (U, C). U*n is a direct sum of 2^n Haar-random n-qubit unitaries indexed by k. C is a classical language built recursively and independently of U, with C(x) = 1 iff x describes a QMA verifier that runs in time at most |x| - 1 and whose U-averaged maximum acceptance probability exceeds 1/2. With probability 1, PromiseBQP = PromiseQMA and PRUs (hence PRS) with n = lambda exist. The oracle field and Statement name this pair. The Notes record the conference version's PSPACE-complete C and its proof error (Sec. 1.5, 5.4, Conj. 34). No readable source shows that DDN91's formally defined scheme meets the game. Still needed: DDN91 (DOI-only) for its formal definition and theorem, plus PR05 or LPV08 (TCC 2008) for a theorem relating DDN-style NM to the view-inclusive game for statistically binding tag-based schemes. The abstract claims 'best-possible' semantic security ('reveals the order of the corresponding plaintexts and nothing else'), with k-bit comparison from a '(k/2+1)-way multilinear map'. It is silent on any idealized model and on the form of the definition. Bun-Zhandry (arXiv 1505.00388v1, §2 before Def. 2.7; Zhandry is a BLR+15 co-author) call BLR+15's notion 'a modification of the standard notion of CPA security ... the left and right sets of challenge messages must have the same order'. They call their own notion, which considers 'only ... static adversaries that submit all queries at once', a relaxation of it. So BLR+15's notion is left-right indistinguishability, presumably with adaptive queries. Still to confirm from BLR+15 itself: the definition, and whether the security theorem is proved in an idealized (generic multilinear-map) model. - [ ] [Math] `searchable-symmetric-encryption`: check CGKO06's trace and view definitions for document identifiers. The page gives each document an arbitrary identifier `$\mathrm{id}(D_j)$` and has the server return ciphertexts by identifier, but the server holds only $(I, \mathbf{c})$ and nothing links `$c_j$` to `$\mathrm{id}(D_j)$` (Syntax, last paragraph). The CGKO06 trace is given as `$\calL_1(\mathbf{D}) = (|D_1|, \ldots, |D_n|)$`, with no identifiers. Identifiers reach the leakage only through the access patterns in `$\calL_2$`, after queries. So if SSE-2's index stores identifiers in the clear (believed, unverified against CGKO06), then in the adaptive game a simulator that produces $I_1$ from `$\calL_1$` alone cannot reproduce the adversary-chosen identifiers, and the Other-results claim that SSE-2 is adaptively secure for the CGKO06 trace fails under the page's own game. If CGKO06's trace and view contain `$\mathrm{id}(D_1), \ldots, \mathrm{id}(D_n)$`, add them to `$\calL_1$` and label each `$c_j$` with `$\mathrm{id}(D_j)$` in the Syntax paragraph. If CGKO06 take identifiers to be positions, state `$\mathrm{id}(D_j) = j$` in the Syntax. Checked 2026-11-10: the only copy reachable here is eprint 2006/210, whose PDF is behind the bot check; arXiv has no copy. Its abstract does not define the trace or view, so the page is unchanged — _source: content/Primitives/searchable-symmetric-encryption.md_

- [ ] [FactCheck] The Encrypt-with-Hash Statement ('PRIV-secure D-PKE for every min-entropy μ = ω(log λ)') is false under the wiki's ROM PRIV definition, in which both A*m and A_g query H and min-entropy is taken over H too. The counterexample has been checked: v=1, A_m outputs x = H(0)‖r' for a uniform bit r', t = r', so Pr[x = m] ≤ 2^{-|H(0)|}; A_g queries H(0), recomputes both candidate ciphertexts, and gets Adv^priv = 1/2 when the PKE is perfectly correct. BBO07's condition is still unread: the eprint 2006/186 PDF is behind the bot check, there is no arXiv version, and the abstract is silent. - [ ] [FactCheck] The Even–Mansour bullet in '# Other results' ('is broken by every attack making q queries to E^{±1} and p queries to P^{±1} with probability at most O(pq/2^n)') still names no event. EM91 and its journal version (JC:EveMan97) are DOI-only and unread, and so is KR96. Secondary sources point to EM's existential-forgery or cracking goals, not indistinguishability. Hou17 (arXiv 1707.01699v2, §3.1, Definition 12) restates EM's two notions. The first is the Existential Forgery Problem: with oracles E*k, D_k, P, P^{-1}, output a pair (m, c) with E_k(m) = c that was not queried. The second is the Cracking Problem. Hou17 also proves the one-key group analogue of the EM bound, O(st/|G|) (Theorem 13). GGN14 (arXiv 1409.0421v3) says EM bound the queries needed to 'decrypt a new message'. ABKM21 (arXiv 2112.07530v1) instead calls it 'roughly' indistinguishability. DKS12's abstract says EM's 'lower bound proof considered known plaintexts' (T = Ω(2^n/D)) and that the single-key scheme 'provides exactly the same security'. Still to do: (1) read EM91 to confirm the forgery/cracking goal and reword the sentence to name it; (2) align the DKS12 single-key sentence with the same goal; (3) for an sPRP statement, Hou17 Theorem 17 proves Adv = O(st/|G|) for one-key EM over any group, including XOR. Hou17 is not in cryptobib, so it would need an inline-bibtex reference page first; alternatively, read KR96/KR01 — \_source: content/Primitives/pseudorandom-permutation.md*

- [ ] [FactCheck] Still open (2026-11-10): the Sparse LPN definition cannot hold as stated for constant d, the standard regime (arXiv:2605.10056v2 §1). At m = 2 the two rows coincide with probability 1/C(k,d), and then v*1+v_2 = e_1+e_2, giving advantage (1-2eps)^2/(2·C(k,d)). Dao–Jain (arXiv:2402.03633v1 §1, §2.3; CRYPTO 2024, cryptobib C:DaoJai24) confirm it: a uniformly random constant-sparsity matrix has a constant-weight kernel vector with inverse-polynomial probability, and then 'one cannot hope for distinguishing security'. The remedies are o(1) advantage [ABW10] or a 'good' matrix distribution [AK19]. GHJS25 (arXiv:2611.11701v1, Conjecture 4.3) does not state F_2 Sparse LPN. It conjectures noisy k-LIN over F_p for prime p = n^{Omega(1)}, with row weight Omega(log n), for every (gamma, Omega(log n), 2^{(log n)^alpha})-expanding matrix with nonzero entries uniform in F_p^\*, and noise rate (log n)^{-c}, requiring only distinguishing advantage at most 1/4 (1/16 in Conjecture 1.3). Still to do: read how BCM+25 (ePrint 2025/1644) and CHKV25 (ePrint 2024/1760) state Sparse LPN. Neither is on arXiv, the abstracts are silent and the PDFs are bot-checked. Then restate '(k,eps,d)-Sparse LPN is hard if for all efficient A and all polynomials m' to match, splitting it into separate variants if the two papers differ, and add a DJ24 reference stub from C:DaoJai24 to cite the caveat. — \_source: content/Assumptions/learning-parity-with-noise.md*

- [ ] [FactCheck] PRV12 is still checked only against its abstract (eprint 2012/653; the PDF and the 2010 thesis cannot be reached). Open: (a) whether the attack also breaks selective-ID IBE; (b) whether it runs in polynomial time or is only query-efficient; (c) which assumptions 'DDH (or similar)' covers, in particular CDH; (d) whether the result needs a prime-order group, as the Statement and rationale say (the abstract does not); (e) how PRV12's model differs from Shoup's. On (e): the abstract describes PRV12's model as 'oracle access to the group operation of randomly labelled group elements', while the Statement says Shoup's model is the one 'in which schemes also see random labels'. Zha22's abstract (eprint 2022/226) says only that the known separations 'do not fully apply to Shoup'. Known from the Schul-Ganz–Segev abstract (ITC 2021, eprint 2021/745): PRV12 rule out generic-group IBE supporting an identity space of sufficiently large polynomial size. Schul-Ganz–Segev tighten this to at most $n_{pp}$ identities for $n_{pp}$ group elements in the public parameters. That paper may answer the scope questions once read, and could become a further source or a Notes bullet for the barrier (no reference page yet) — _source: content/Barriers/no-ddh-to-ibe-prv12.md_

- [ ] [Math] CJJ21 is recorded from `polynomial-ratio-lwe`, still unchecked against the paper. The only readable source, the eprint abstract (2021/808; PDF behind the bot check; not on arXiv), says security 'relies on the hardness of the Learning with Errors (LWE) problem against polynomial-time adversaries' and gives BARGs for NP 'based solely on the LWE assumption'. That is polynomial hardness, not a polynomial modulus-to-noise ratio, so it settles no LWE variant. Confirm from CJJ21's main theorems (SNARG for P, BARG for NP) that a polynomial ratio suffices. If a larger ratio is needed, retarget both pages to that variant (e.g. `superpolynomial-ratio-lwe`). `lwe` is no fallback, because `polynomial-ratio-lwe ⇒ lwe` would derive the same edge. If CJJ21's correlation-intractable hash is PS19's, that ingredient is 'based on plain LWE (with small polynomial approximation factors)' (PS19 eprint abstract); the other LWE-based components are unchecked. The same theorems should settle what the abstract does not state: adaptive soundness and public verifiability of the SNARG, the BARG's soundness for statements fixed before the CRS, its somewhere extractability and proof size, the Fiat–Shamir `via`, and the SNARG page's polynomial security loss, whose rationale is inferred from the abstract — _source: content/Reductions/polynomial-ratio-lwe-to-snarg-for-p-cjj21.md, content/Reductions/polynomial-ratio-lwe-to-barg-for-np-cjj21.md_
- [ ] [Math] BKM20's LPN noise rate is still unconfirmed. The eprint abstract (https://eprint.iacr.org/2020/258) names only "the learning parity with noise (LPN) assumption" and gives no rate. Hardness at that rate is mid-noise LPN (`lpn-mid-noise`, $k^{-\gamma}$ with $1/2 \le \gamma < 1$), a weaker assumption than `lpn-low-noise` ($\log^c(k)/k$, $c > 1$). Confirm the rate and its quantifier on $c$ against BKM20's main theorem (eprint PDF). If it is $k^{-c}$, move the hypothesis to `lpn-mid-noise` or to a new variant recording the exact rate, keeping the id (a node refinement on the same theorem). — _source: content/Reductions/lpn-low-noise-and-ddh-to-nizk-argument-for-np-bkm20.md_

- [ ] [Math] BDV17 also rule out fully-black-box constructions of hard languages in NP ∩ coNP from iO + injective OWF. This is recorded only in the Notes because there is no NP ∩ coNP-hardness node. Abstracts read 2026-11-10: BDV17's eprint abstract (2016/574, revised 2021) says the primitives 'do not imply hard problems in NP∩coNP or SZK via black-box reductions' and does not name injective OWFs. Ghosal et al. (ePrint 2023/1972, STOC 2023) write that 'Bitansky et al. (SICOMP, 2021) ruled out such a construction based on an injective one-way function ... even with a black-box use of indistinguishability obfuscation'. Still open, and it needs the BDV17 full text (the eprint PDF is behind the bot check, the paper is not on arXiv, and ECCC is unreachable): (a) whether the theorem is worst-case; (b) whether it covers only perfectly correct constructions; (c) whether the CRYPTO 2017 version already has the injective-OWF form or only the SIAM J. Comput. 2021 version does (if only the journal version, the Note's credit needs a reference page for that version, and cryptobib has no entry for it); (d) two claims on the same page that the abstract does not cover: that the SZK separation is worst-case (the Statement has an adversary decide Statistical Difference relative to Ψ on every instance), and that deniable encryption is among the primitives covered (the abstract names public-key encryption, oblivious transfer and functional encryption, 'and many others') — _source: content/Barriers/no-io-and-owp-to-szk-hardness-bdv17.md_
- [ ] [Math] No reduction page records statistically rerandomizable encryption ⇒ average-case-hard SZK (BL13). Abstracts read 2026-11-10: BL13's eprint abstract (2013/344, also ECCC TR12-156) gives the meta-reduction limits and the conversion of a strong homomorphic evaluator into a rerandomizer ('statistically close to being independent and identically distributed with the original one'). It does not give the SZK membership of distinguishing encryptions of 0 from 1, which the third Note on the BDV17 barrier and the Note on he-to-re-bl13 attribute to BL13. BDV17's eprint abstract (2016/574) says 'For some cryptographic primitives, such as one-way permutations and homomorphic encryption, we know that the answer is yes — they imply hard problems in NP∩coNP and SZK, respectively', without naming the paper or the homomorphic notion. Still open, and it needs the BL13 body (eprint PDF behind the bot check, ECCC unreachable, not on arXiv): the membership theorem and its exact hypotheses. Also unresolved is the promise issue: `szk-hardness` requires the sampler to land in the promise on every draw, but the natural sampler misses it with negligible probability. - [ ] [Math] OW93 (DOI-only) is still unread. Still unknown: its exact average-case notion (distribution class, error bound) and whether its auxiliary-input hardness is negligible or 1/poly. Secondary sources answer part of the rest. On the argument: arXiv 2602.16156v1 §1.1 gives OW93's candidate for NIZK as f*x(ρ) = the CRS output by Sim(x; ρ), inverted distributionally, with IL89 turning distributional OWFs into OWFs. arXiv 2602.17651v1 §2.4 says OW93's techniques, extended to k-round protocols, need εs + k·εzk < 1, and its footnote 9 links the O(k)·εzk term in OW93's analysis to applying zero knowledge message by message rather than to the whole transcript. In both overviews the decider is efficient, so computational ZK is used directly; no computational-to-statistical step appears there, and this item's framing of the argument may be wrong. That is not enough to write a Sketch for the two OW93 pages. On attribution: AR16 (eprint 2016/519 abstract) credits Ost91 with SZK ⊄ BPP ⇒ auxiliary-input OWF, and arXiv 2602.16156v1 §1 credits OW93 with the CZK worst-case case. These do not conflict, since SZK ⊆ CZK, but Ost91 itself (IEEE-only) is still needed to confirm Ost91's priority. The Ost91 page Note citing OW93 for the SZK worst-case result is true as stated. — \_source: content/Reductions/zkp-to-hash-function.md, content/Reductions/czk-hardness-to-owf-ow93.md, content/Reductions/szk-hardness-to-owf-ost91.md*

This bound also applies to offline ORAMs and constructions with statistical security [8]', citing BN16. If GO96 itself proves only the perfect-security online case, add BN16 to the barrier's source. The full version (arXiv 2105.01417v1, App. A.1) defines PIR with client error ν(n) negligible, and Corollary 65 bounds O(n)-security-parameter-expanding fully-black-box constructions from TDPs, with the database size n as the only security parameter. Added `bar-tdp-to-polylog-cpir-hhs08` and its enhanced-TDP sibling `bar-enhanced-trapdoor-permutation-to-polylog-cpir-hhs08`. They cover only constructions that are O(n)-expanding when run with λ = n^{1/d}; a construction that is polynomially preserving in λ is not covered — _source: content/Barriers/no-tdp-to-one-round-sublinear-cpir-hhs08.md_

Files and ids are kept and no id is retired, since both STARK and pcs are non-interactive nodes. Support: the BCS16 abstract ("maps any public-coin IOP into a non-interactive proof in the random oracle model", "unconditional ... in the random oracle model") and the wiki's BCS16 page. The interactive collision-resistance claim was dropped from the PCS page (it was in the Statement and rationale.model) and not re-added. The STARK page keeps its existing Note on it. Both stay unchecked under ffr:verification-gap-20, because the BBHR18a abstract (eprint 2018/046) is silent and its PDF is unread. - [ ] [Math] BCCT13's eprint abstract (2012/095) puts collision-resistant hashing on the bootstrapping theorem as a whole ("assuming collision-resistant hashing, any SNARK ... can be bootstrapped"). It describes the first step only as recursive composition into a "weak" PCD system for shallow distributed computations, and does not say whether the later depth reduction to path PCD/IVC uses CRH. This needs the paper's depth-reduction theorem (eprint PDF unread, not on arXiv). If that theorem assumes CRH, SNARK ⇒ Recursive SNARKs (red-snark-to-recursive-snarks, conclusion incremental-verifiable-computation) needs crhf as a second hypothesis, or should conclude only constant-depth PCD — _source: content/Reductions/snark-to-recursive-snarks.md_
- [ ] [Math] BCIOP13 (eprint 2012/718) and BISW17 (eprint 2017/240): neither abstract states the soundness flavour of the compiled designated-verifier SNARGs (adaptive or non-adaptive, with or without a verification oracle), so both stay recorded as the non-adaptive designated-verifier-snarg. BISW17's says the argument size is "quasilinear in the security parameter" and its linear PCPs are "over small finite fields", consistent with the variant's arbitrary prime $p$. - [x] [FactCheck] GOS06a: per its abstract, Cao–Liu (ePrint 2018/327) concern a prover holding the CRS trapdoor, which would not contradict computational soundness; check whether the note claims anything beyond that before treating GOS06a as disputed, and that the theorem rests on the BGN05 subgroup decision assumption as the bilinear-map page defines it — verified 2026-11-10: Cao–Liu (arXiv 1805.06351v1, retitled; same authors as ePrint 2018/327, and its §1 repeats that abstract's argument) attack only a prover that knows the factorization $p, q$ of the CRS modulus (§3.2). They concede that 'the system seems secure if the trapdoor key is indeed not accessible to the prover' (§4). Their claim that the problems 'remain open' rests on the objection that a trusted CRS generator is 'incompatible with the general model of zero-knowledge proof' (§3.2), which is aimed at the CRS model, not at GOS06a's theorem in it. Their §3.2 example is not a false claim either: with $\alpha_1 = kq = 1 + \ell p$, $c/g = g^{\ell p} h^{\alpha_2}$ lies in the order-$q$ subgroup $\langle h \rangle$, so $c$ commits to 1. No wiki page cites the note. Its §§1–2 review says the GOS scheme rests on the BGN05 subgroup decision problem in a composite-order symmetric bilinear group $(p, q, \GG, \GG_T, e, g)$, with binding key $h = g^{px}$ of order $q$ and hiding key $h = g^x$. That matches the bilinear-map page's game with $q_1 := q$, $q_2 := p$; GOS also require $p < q$ and efficient membership testing. This support is secondary: GOS06a's own text (eprint PDF) was not read — _source: content/Reductions/subgroup-decision-to-nonadaptive-nizk-argument-for-np-gos06a.md_
- [ ] [FactCheck] BP04 (eprint 2004/008; no arXiv version): the abstract confirms that HT98's 3-round, negligible-error ZK arguments for NP rest on KEA1 and KEA2, that KEA2 is false, and that BP04 'recover these results ... under a suitably modified new assumption called KEA3'. The following are still unread and known here only from a thesis summary: the KEA3 game (input $(g, g^a, g^b, g^{ab})$, extractor output $(c_1, c_2)$ with $C = g^{c_1}(g^b)^{c_2}$); the group setting (the order-$q$ subgroup of $\ZZ_p^*$ for a safe prime, or any prime-order group with checkable membership); whether DLOG hardness is a separate hypothesis; and whether BP04 modify the HT98 protocol, since the abstract says only that they recover the results — _source: content/Assumptions/knowledge-of-exponent.md, content/Reductions/prime-order-dlog-and-kea3-to-three-message-zk-argument-bp04.md_

- [x] [Math] The snark node is "a succinct argument system for a relation R", so as a hypothesis (BCCT13 bootstrapping, SNARK ⇒ Recursive SNARKs, SNARK ⇒ NIZK) it admits trivial-relation instances; state it as preprocessing SNARKs for circuit satisfiability with every circuit $C$ an input to $\Setup$ — fixed 2026-11-10: the snark node is now a preprocessing SNARK for circuit satisfiability. Every Boolean circuit C is an input to Setup, which runs in time poly(λ,|C|), and completeness, knowledge soundness and succinctness are quantified over all poly-size circuit families. This matches Gro16's 'pairing-based (preprocessing) SNARK for arithmetic circuit satisfiability' (eprint 2016/260 abstract) and the preprocessing SNARKs that BCCT13 bootstrap (eprint 2012/095 abstract). A '— standard' remark carries adaptively knowledge-sound R*U arguments into the node, given a collision-resistant hash of C. Still open, as separate follow-ups: KMY20's proof-size hypothesis on snark-to-nizk, and the missing CRHF on the R_U-based producing edges — \_source: content/Primitives/succinct-argument.md*

The Notes of content/Reductions/prime-order-dlog-to-interactive-succinct-argument-bccgp16.md:30 already credit the logarithmic-opening scheme to BCMS20, so the two pages disagree. - [ ] [Math] MPSW19 and BN06, still unread (checked 2026-11-10). Neither is on arXiv. MPSW19's PDF (eprint 2018/068) is behind the bot check and BN06 is DOI-only. MPSW19's abstract (https://eprint.iacr.org/2018/068) says only 'provably secure in the plain public-key model'. Still open: whether their plain-model unforgeability games exclude the forgery message $m^*$ or only the pair $(L^*, m^*)$. § Unforgeability in the plain public-key model cites MPSW19 for the message-only condition, which the BLS-based schemes (Bol03, RY07, BDN18) meet. If MPSW19 uses the pair condition, the game's attribution to MPSW19 is wrong and MuSig's theorem is stronger than recorded: correct the attribution and note the stronger notion. If BN06 uses it, the BDN18 page's 'plain public-key model of BN06' names a stronger game than the one the wiki records. — _source: content/Primitives/multi-signature.md_
- [ ] [Math] RY07, still unread (checked 2026-11-10). It is not on arXiv, and the eprint 2007/264 PDF is behind the bot check. The abstract (https://eprint.iacr.org/2007/264) says only that 'simple POP protocols yield provable security of Boldyreva's multisignature scheme' and that a POP 'can be as simple as just a signature over the certificate request message'. It does not say which POP Boldyreva's multisignature is registered with (a BLS signature on the public key under a separate hash is the usual reading), nor the group setting and assumption of RY07's theorem. The hypotheses [type-1-pairing, cdh] are the special case of Bol03's gap Diffie–Hellman setting, supported only by a later restatement that RY07's security rests on CDH. Once RY07 is read, write the POP into the Statement and confirm or retarget the hypotheses. — _source: content/Reductions/type-1-pairing-and-cdh-to-multi-signature-pop-ry07.md_

Confirm that BDN18's co-CDH is the isomorphism-free form $(g_1^\alpha, g_1^\beta, g_2^\beta) \mapsto g_1^{\alpha\beta}$. That form was taken from a restatement of the BDN note and from BDN18 defining a separate $\psi$-co-CDH with an oracle for $\psi$. If BDN18 prove the BLS multi-signature under the $\psi$ form, retarget the hypothesis to co-cdh. That is only exact for pairings with an efficiently computable $\psi$, the setting of the wiki's co-cdh. — _source: content/Reductions/co-cdh-star-to-multi-signature-bdn18.md, content/Assumptions/co-computational-diffie-hellman.md_
